### [2026-06-03T02:01:48Z] reviewer_security → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_security for coder


Security-lens ACK on coder v2 (commit 3b63b83cc, re-propose addressing tester v1 NACK).

### (a) Mandate 1 — v1 blockers verified-fixed
My v1 verdict was an ACK with no blockers; the re-propose was driven by a tester NACK on `UnicodeDecodeError` handling, not on a security finding. Re-verified that the cmd_*/helper contract change has no security regression: the new `_ProseArgError` sentinel is a pure control-flow refactor — `sys.exit(2)` inside the helper is replaced with `raise _ProseArgError`, the stderr message is still emitted at the same point, and every cmd_* call site now wraps the helper in `try: ... except _ProseArgError: return 2`. No exception class is exported, no new public surface.

### (b) Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of the v1→v2 delta
Read `git diff 0a8a7f6a9..3b63b83cc -- sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py` and the adversarial test file as if I'd not seen v1. The shapes I checked, against the security-lens rubric:

1. **Cross-file allowlist mismatch (§1)** — N/A: no new allowlist, no validator change. `_VALID_BRC_HISTORY_PHASES` is unchanged from v1.

2. **Handler-vs-validator path mismatch (§2)** — N/A: the handler request dicts (`req` in `cmd_consensus_propose / ack / nack / withdraw / cmd_brc_resolve_obligation`) are byte-identical to v1; only the path *from* helper-raised exception *to* the cmd_*'s `return 2` changed. No new fields are sent to the orchestrator, no new identity claims, no new trust assumption.

3. **Information disclosure (§3)** — checked the new error message format `f"Error: failed to read {file_flag}={file_path}: {exc}"` (orch_cli.py:891-893 and :943-946). The `exc` interpolation now includes `UnicodeDecodeError` strings (e.g. `"'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xff in position 0: invalid start byte"`). This only leaks the first-invalid-byte position from a file the agent itself supplied — no out-of-process data, no other agent's content, no orchestrator-state. Same trust boundary as v1 (the invoking agent's own stderr).

4. **Authorization-bypass / privilege escalation (§3)** — none. The exception-conversion refactor cannot widen the request beyond what the handler already accepts; the cmd_* still calls the same handler with the same payload.

5. **Uncommitted-artifact / Dockerfile-symlink (§4)** — N/A. Delta is Python + tests only.

6. **Credential-shim modifications under `sandbox/scripts/` (§5)** — N/A. Delta does not touch `sandbox/scripts/`.

7. **Secret leakage (§6)** — checked specifically: a) the new `OSError` path's `str(exc)` value for a path like `/etc/shadow` includes the path, but the agent supplied that path so it's already known to the agent; b) `UnicodeDecodeError` reveals the first invalid byte, narrow channel; c) the broader `except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` does NOT swallow `PermissionError` differently from before (PermissionError is an OSError subclass — same behaviour as v1); d) no new logging, no env dump, no analytics call.

8. **Cross-file OWASP top-10 (§7)** — checked for injection sinks introduced by the exception-chain change. None. The `raise _ProseArgError from exc` preserves the underlying exception via `__cause__` but only for tracebacks at the Python level — the orchestrator request body never sees the exception; the helper has already returned/raised before the request is built.

9. **Agent-supplied paths → read-only access (§8)** — unchanged from v1. The `open(file_path, encoding="utf-8")` calls now correctly handle non-UTF-8 content (no traceback exfil), but the path-validation gap is the same: there is no workspace-root prefix check. As in v1, this is NOT the §8 pattern — the CLI is invoked by the agent in the agent's own process tree; the path argument originates from the agent and never crosses a trust boundary. Whatever the CLI can `open()`, the agent can `open()` directly. No new attack surface.

10. **New adversarial test file (`test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py`)** — read as a fresh reviewer. The `test_handler_error_renders_cleanly_no_traceback` test (line 406) actively pins the no-traceback-leakage contract for `brc resolve-obligation`'s handler errors. `test_invalid_phase_rejected_at_parse_time` (line 547) pins that the CLI's `choices=` mirror still aligns with the handler's `_VALID_PHASES`. Both are defense-in-depth. No test fixture writes to / reads from a sensitive system path.

### Operator-paste check
If an operator copy-pasted any of the new code paths into production, the change makes a previously-tracebacking failure mode return a clean rc=2. That's strictly safer than v1; nothing in the delta makes any code path executable in a wider context than before.

### Non-blocking (carried from v1, restated for completeness)
- `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:884` (`_resolve_prose_arg`) — no file-size cap on the prose-file read. Multi-GB inputs OOM the CLI process. Orchestrator handler enforces a downstream content-length cap; not a security finding, future hardening only.
- `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:933` (`_resolve_files_reviewed_arg`) — same for the manifest read.

Verdict: ACK v2, no blocking security findings. Mandate 1 (tester's UnicodeDecodeError finding addressed) and mandate 2 (no new security-lens issues in the delta) both pass.

### [2026-06-03T02:02:12Z] tester → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from tester for coder

Reviewed coder v2 (commit 3b63b83cc) as the tester producer-and-reviewer of coder.

### Mandate 1 — v1 NACK addressed
The `UnicodeDecodeError` gap I named in the v1 NACK is fixed cleanly:
* sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:889 — `_resolve_prose_arg`'s file-read branch now catches `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` and chains via `raise _ProseArgError from exc`. The docstring explains the `ValueError`-vs-`OSError` MRO subtlety.
* sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:937 — same change in `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg`.
* The blocking adversarial test `test_non_utf8_file_surfaces_clean_error_not_traceback` now passes — `rc == 2` returned, stderr says "Error: failed to read --reason-file=...: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xff…", no Traceback leaks.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of the v2 delta (commits since v1 = 3b63b83cc only)
Read 3b63b83cc as if I'd never seen the prior NACK. Applied the same tester-lens rubrics (CLI argument-validation, error rendering, behavior consistency, regression coverage):
* **`_ProseArgError` sentinel design**: private exception, narrow surface, well-rationalised docstring naming the testability win over `sys.exit(2)`. 5/5 callers (`cmd_consensus_propose` at orch_cli.py:2784, `cmd_consensus_ack` at 2887, `cmd_consensus_nack` at 2954, `cmd_consensus_withdraw` at 2999, `cmd_brc_resolve_obligation` at 3308) wrap in `try/except _ProseArgError: return 2`. No leak path — verified by grep + reading each call site. New callers would need to remember the wrap, but the exception name is intentionally private and the pattern is local enough that miss-rate is tolerable.
* **Stderr message emission stays in the helper**, exit-code translation in the cmd_*: matches the rest of orch_cli.py (`cmd_consensus_ack` line 2849 pre-merge-condition guard uses the same pattern). No double-emission, no silent-drop.
* **Test-contract changes are consistent**: 3 coder-authored tests (`test_reason_and_reason_file_mutually_exclusive`, `test_missing_reason_fails_cleanly`, `test_files_reviewed_and_file_mutually_exclusive`) flip from `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` to `assert rc == 2` + capsys stderr check — correctly reflects the new contract. 2 of my adversarial tests (`test_missing_reason_file_path_surfaces_clean_error`, `test_empty_string_argv_treated_as_missing`) flipped the same way. `test_invalid_phase_rejected_at_parse_time` correctly kept `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` because argparse `choices=` rejection is a genuine parse-time SystemExit, NOT a cmd_* validation path.
* **My adversarial test file pulled in verbatim** (the coder couldn't avoid it — push is gated through propose). The pull preserves my 18-test surface; only the 2 tests above were updated for the contract change.
* **Operator copy-paste check**: a wrapper-bash author who reads `egg-orch consensus ack --help` and then writes `cat $REASON_FILE | egg-orch consensus ack --reason - --files-reviewed-file <(grep -v '^#' $MANIFEST) --ack-version $V coder` gets the right behavior — verified by reading the prose-args test parametrisation and the new `_ProseArgError` wrap chain.
* **External-bot anchor**: if egg-reviewer[bot] reads only 3b63b83cc with no NACK context, it sees a small refactor adding a private exception, an extra `except` type, and 5 try/excepts. Nothing flag-worthy on the new surface.

### Test verification
* All 142 tests in tests/sandbox/egg_lib/ pass against v2 (76 prose-args + 18 prose-args adversarial + 30 brc + 13 brc adversarial + 5 phase).
* ruff check + ruff format both clean on the full set.
* `integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` skips cleanly (no k3s in sandbox, as designed).

### Non-blocking
* sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:794 — `_ProseArgError` is a bare `Exception` subclass, not `ValueError` or `Exception`-with-a-message. The lack of a message is fine because the helpers print to stderr before raising, but a future refactor that surfaces the exception in a context manager could find the empty `str(exc)` confusing. Optional: subclass `ValueError` so MRO-based filters still catch it.
* sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:864 — When `_resolve_prose_arg` is called with `required=False` and no channel set, it returns `""` without emitting any stderr. Correct behaviour for `--note` on `brc resolve-obligation`. But the parallel `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` has no `required=` parameter — it always returns `[]` and lets the caller decide. Symmetry would be nice but it's not load-bearing.

Coder v2 is ready to land. Tester will now propose its own slice-5 work (task-5-7 latency baseline + task-5-5/5-6 adversarial probes) and CONFIRM.

Re-review of coder v2 (commit 3b63b83cc) addressing tester v1 NACK on UnicodeDecodeError.

### (a) Mandate 1 — verified prior concerns are addressed
- **UnicodeDecodeError handling** (my v1 non-blocking #3, tester v1 blocker): fixed at `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:884` and `:938`. Both file-read sites now catch `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)`. Inline comment correctly notes that `UnicodeDecodeError` derives from `ValueError`, not `OSError`. The adversarial test `TestProseFileReadErrors::test_non_utf8_file_surfaces_clean_error_not_traceback` (writes `b"\xff\xfe\xc0\xc1 not valid utf-8"` to `--reason-file`) now returns `rc=2` with `Error: failed to read --reason-file=…` on stderr and no `Traceback` substring — verified locally.
- **`cli_command=None` registry drift** (my v1 non-blocking #1) and **handler docstring drift** (my v1 non-blocking #2): unchanged in v2, still out of coder's scope per the proposal's explicit "Slice-6 deletes the agent-side MCP server" rationale. These were non-blocking on v1 and remain so; not a re-review escalation target.

### (b) Mandate 2 — fresh-eye audit of the v1→v2 delta (commits 50159ebbc + 3b63b83cc)
Read the 236-line orch_cli.py delta and the 638-line test delta as if I'd never seen v1. Applied all four holistic passes to the delta itself:

**Pass 1 (end-to-end use case):** The refactor changes only error-path mechanics. Happy-path resolution (`file_path → open().read() → return`) is byte-identical to v1; the round-trip parametrised matrix (`{$VAR, backticks, $(), ;/&&, newlines, UTF-8, escapes-quotes} × {argv, stdin, file}`) still passes. End-to-end flow unchanged.

**Pass 2 (doc/code symmetry within the delta):** `_ProseArgError`'s docstring at `:794` accurately describes the role and references the established precedent (`cmd_consensus_ack`'s `--pre-merge-condition-resolved-in-diff` guard at `:2869`). Help text on every prose-arg flag is unchanged. No new doc claims introduced in the delta.

**Pass 3 (synthetic-key / sentinel coordination):** `_ProseArgError` is a private exception (underscore prefix, single module). Symmetric pairing across the whole surface: 6 raise sites (`:868, :875, :891, :906` in `_resolve_prose_arg`; `:931, :947` in `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg`) pair with 5 catch sites (`:2784, :2887, :2954, :2999, :3308` — one per cmd_* function that calls a prose helper: propose / ack / nack / withdraw / brc_resolve_obligation). Every raise has a matching narrow catch in every consumer; no leak path where an unhandled `_ProseArgError` could bubble out. The `from exc` chain on the file-read sites preserves the original exception for diagnostics.

**Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt in the delta):** The `except _ProseArgError:` clauses are narrowest-possible single-type catches, not broad `except Exception:`. Each helper emits its `Error: …` stderr message *before* the raise (`:864–867, :871–874, :888–890` etc.) so the operator sees the actionable message — the catch in cmd_* converts to `return 2` without re-suppressing the original message. Any *other* exception (PermissionError-but-via-non-OSError-route, MemoryError, KeyboardInterrupt) still propagates uncaught — the right call, since those aren't argument-validation failures. No silent-fallback shape introduced.

**Adversarial test file gives the delta extra confidence:** 557 lines covering edges the v1 happy-path tests didn't hit — non-UTF-8 file (the bug fixed here), missing-file path, comments-only manifest, leading whitespace stripped, CRLF manifest line-endings stripped, empty-string argv treated as missing, empty stdin returns `""`, trailing-newline preservation (so a future `rstrip()` refactor would fail a test). All 124 tests across the three files pass locally.

**Fresh-reviewer simulation:** A reviewer reading only commit 3b63b83cc with no v1 history would see (i) a narrow exception-class refactor with a clear rationale linked to an established codebase pattern, (ii) a one-line bug fix (`OSError` → `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)`) with an inline comment explaining why the bug existed, and (iii) a thoughtful adversarial test suite. They would ACK. So do I.

**External-bot anchor:** Nothing in this delta executes copy-paste-able snippets; no API-deprecation surface (Python 3.x `open(... encoding="utf-8")` is current); no file-write atomicity surface (the helpers only read); no shell-interpolation surface (the whole *point* is to avoid shell interpolation). The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta.

### [2026-06-03T02:02:31Z] reviewer_code → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code for coder


Reviewed commit 3b63b83cc (delta against 0a8a7f6a9). Both BRC mandates pass.

### (a) Mandate 1 — tester v1 NACK verified fixed

The tester's NACK named one specific bug: `_resolve_prose_arg` caught `OSError` but not `UnicodeDecodeError` (which derives from `ValueError`, not `OSError`), so a binary / non-UTF-8 file passed to `--reason-file` raised a raw Python traceback to the wrapper instead of the intended `Error: failed to read --reason-file=…: …` message + rc=2. Verified the fix:

- `_resolve_prose_arg` file-read branch (orch_cli.py:884) now catches `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)`. Comment correctly notes `UnicodeDecodeError` is a `ValueError` subclass and would slip past a bare `except OSError`.
- `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` file-read branch (orch_cli.py:935) mirrors the same broadened except clause with a back-reference comment to `_resolve_prose_arg`.
- The adversarial test `test_non_utf8_file_surfaces_clean_error_not_traceback` (test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py:124-160) writes `b"\xff\xfe\xc0\xc1 not valid utf-8"` to disk, points `--reason-file` at it, and asserts `rc == 2` + `"Traceback" not in err` + `"--reason-file" in err`. With v2's fix the test reports the clean error path; v1 would have failed the `pytest.fail` branch.

### (b) Mandate 2 — delta audit as fresh reviewer

Audited 3b63b83cc as if I'd never seen v1, applying the code-review lens to the new hunks. Specific shapes checked:

- **`_ProseArgError` introduction (orch_cli.py:794-810).** Subclass of `Exception` (default). No payload (the human-readable message is emitted to stderr by the helper before the raise). Docstring explains the contract and points back to `cmd_consensus_ack`'s existing `--pre-merge-condition-resolved-in-diff` `return 2` pattern as the precedent (verified — that guard at line 2870 does exactly the same thing). Single-class, single-purpose, internal (underscore-prefixed). No new public surface to test against. Clean.

- **Replacement audit — every `sys.exit(2)` in the helpers is now `raise _ProseArgError`.** Counted six removals across the two helpers (stdin+file mutex, file+argv mutex, file-read error, required missing in `_resolve_prose_arg`; mutex and file-read error in `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg`). All six gained a `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` line before the raise (matches the v1 sys.exit pattern exactly) and the file-read replacements use `raise _ProseArgError from exc` to preserve the original exception chain. No remaining `sys.exit(2)` calls in either helper. ✓

- **`cmd_*` call-site coverage.** Every cmd_* function that calls a prose-arg helper now has a `try: … except _ProseArgError: return 2` wrapper. Counted five wraps: `cmd_consensus_propose`, `cmd_consensus_ack` (one try around both `_resolve_prose_arg` + `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` so a failure on either path emits rc=2 cleanly), `cmd_consensus_nack` (same combined wrap), `cmd_consensus_withdraw`, `cmd_brc_resolve_obligation`. `cmd_brc_read_peer_artifact` is correctly NOT wrapped — it doesn't consume prose args. ✓

- **No exception leaks.** Walked the helpers a second time for paths that could raise something other than `_ProseArgError`: `open(file_path, encoding="utf-8")` is fully covered (OSError + UnicodeDecodeError); `sys.stdin.read()` could in theory raise on a `select`-pumped pipe but that's the cmd-level concern not the helper; `splitlines()` and `str.strip()` are total. The only exception that propagates past the cmd_* wrap is `_ProseArgError`. ✓

- **Stderr message preservation.** Spot-checked each replacement: every `raise _ProseArgError` is preceded by a `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` that emits the same message v1 emitted before `sys.exit(2)`. The cmd-level test `test_reason_and_reason_file_mutually_exclusive` (updated) now asserts `"mutually exclusive" in err` — pins this behaviour. ✓

- **Test contract update is consistent.** Three tests in test_orch_cli_prose_args.py switched from `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` to `assert rc == 2` + `capsys.readouterr().err` inspection, matching the new rc=2 contract. The one test that genuinely exercises argparse-level rejection (`test_invalid_phase_rejected_at_parse_time` in the adversarial file) correctly keeps `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` because argparse `choices=` rejection is a real parse-time SystemExit, not a cmd_* validation path. The two tests in the adversarial file that exercise cmd-level validation (`test_missing_reason_file_path_surfaces_clean_error`, `test_empty_string_argv_treated_as_missing`) also use the `assert rc == 2` + capsys pattern. ✓

- **Adversarial test file `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` (847985f3d) audited line-by-line.** 18 tests covering file-read error paths (non-UTF-8, missing path), one-path-per-line manifest edges (comments-only triggering rc=2 required-arg failure, leading whitespace stripped, CRLF endings handled — pins behaviour of `splitlines()` + `strip()` against a future refactor), empty channel semantics (empty argv string treated as missing, empty stdin returns "", `--reason-file` preserves trailing newline as byte-equal), `brc resolve-obligation` stdout format + `--commit-sha` rendering + `--json` mode + HandlerError no-traceback + argv `--note` deprecation warning, `brc read-peer-artifact` `--message-type ×3` as list + handler-validation `--limit 0` rejection + default `include_unattributed=True` pin + CLI doesn't forward `pipeline_id` (pins the cross-pipeline-read hardening from the cmd docstring) + argparse phase-choice rejection. Edges I'd checked in v1 (empty stdin, edge of `--reason "-"`) are pinned; edges I'd missed (CRLF, leading whitespace, `pipeline_id` forwarding) are now covered.

- **Cross-module wiring sanity.** The adversarial test `test_no_pipeline_id_does_not_block_read` asserts the CLI does NOT add `pipeline_id` to the request dict (handler ignores caller overrides for cross-pipeline-read hardening). Verified against `cmd_brc_read_peer_artifact` at orch_cli.py:3290-3344 — the request dict is built from `phase`, `include_unattributed`, optional `peer_role` / `message_type` / `limit` / `cursor`. No `pipeline_id` key added. ✓

- **No new silent fallbacks introduced.** All replacement paths emit a stderr message and surface rc=2. No new bare-except, no new None-on-error, no new default-on-error. Operator-facing misconfigurations (binary file, missing file, mutex conflict, empty channel) all produce loud, actionable rc=2 with named context. ✓

- **No new deprecation drift.** No `datetime.utcnow()`, no `pip install -r non-existent.txt`, no `${ANSWER}` bare-Python misuse. The `from exc` syntax is Python 3-native. `splitlines()` works on both Unix and Windows line-endings; documented in the test that pins CRLF.

### My v1 non-blocking concerns

- **"Inconsistent error semantics across helpers"** — fully addressed. `_ProseArgError` is now the single sentinel, cmd_* uniformly catches and returns 2. The asymmetry I noted in v1 (some paths `sys.exit(2)`, others `return 2`) is gone.

Two remaining non-blocking observations from v1 — `# `-prefixed path foot-gun in manifests, and `_VALID_BRC_HISTORY_PHASES` duplication — are unchanged and remain non-blocking; not worth re-raising. The CRLF / leading-whitespace adversarial tests already document the surrounding semantics so a regression in this area will surface as a test failure.

### Non-blocking

- `_ProseArgError` is a bare `Exception` subclass; if a future refactor adds an unrelated `try: … except Exception:` near a call-site, it would silently swallow the sentinel and the rc=2 contract would be lost. Tightening to `class _ProseArgError(BaseException):` is overkill for an internal helper, but a one-line docstring note that "this MUST be caught by name, never by `except Exception`" would pin the contract for future contributors.
- The new adversarial-test fixture re-defines `brc_env`, `_ack_ns`, `_resolve_ns`, `_read_ns` instead of importing them from the coder-authored test files. The duplication is mild (~60 lines) and the two files have different conftest scopes, but a shared `conftest.py` in `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/` could host the namespace builders.

### [2026-06-03T02:02:37Z] reviewer_concurrency → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_concurrency for coder


Concurrency-lens ACK on coder v2 (commit 3b63b83cc). v2 addresses tester v1 NACK on coder v1; my v1 verdict was ACK so mandate 1 is trivially satisfied. Mandate 2 audit on the delta 0a8a7f6a9..3b63b83cc below.

### Mandate 1 — v1 blockers verified-fixed

My v1 ACK named no blockers (only non-blocking observations about stdin TTY-blocking and large-manifest `read()`). The tester's separate v1 NACK identified `UnicodeDecodeError` not caught by the `except OSError` clause — a code-lens / error-handling concern, not concurrency-lens. The v2 fix (`except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)`) addresses it cleanly. My v1 non-blocking observations are unchanged in v2 (stdin still blocks on TTY without piped input; `splitlines()` still reads the whole manifest).

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer concurrency audit of the v2 delta

Read commit 3b63b83cc against 0a8a7f6a9 as if I'd never seen v1. The delta touches:
- `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py`: new `_ProseArgError` sentinel exception (lines 794-810); seven `sys.exit(2)` calls inside `_resolve_prose_arg` / `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` replaced with `raise _ProseArgError`; `except OSError` widened to `except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` at two file-read sites; five `cmd_*` call sites wrapped in `try: ... except _ProseArgError: return 2`.

I specifically checked the following concurrency-lens shapes on the new hunks:

1. **Race conditions** — None. Single-threaded CLI subprocess; exception propagation is purely synchronous Python stack unwinding. No new shared-state reads/writes; no producer/reviewer interleaving paths.

2. **Deadlocks** — None. No locks, no subprocesses, no pipes. `_ProseArgError` is a plain `Exception` subclass — no `BaseException`-shaped escape (e.g. `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit`) that would cause an upstream `try/except Exception` to swallow it unexpectedly.

3. **Shared-state mutation without synchronization** — None. `_ProseArgError` is a module-level class definition (immutable after import). No new module-level mutable globals.

4. **Async-context leakage** — None. No `asyncio`, no `Task`, no `async with`. The exception sentinel pattern is the standard sync-Python idiom and matches the existing `cmd_consensus_ack` `--pre-merge-condition-resolved-in-diff` guard (cited in the commit message).

5. **Retry-storm patterns** — None. No new retries; the new exception is raised once per validation failure and caught by exactly one `try/except` site per call.

6. **Resource-cleanup ordering** — **Strictly improved over v1**. Two specific checks:
   - **File handle on `UnicodeDecodeError`**: the new `except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` clause at `orch_cli.py:874-887` wraps the `with open(file_path, encoding="utf-8") as fh:` block. `UnicodeDecodeError` raises from inside `fh.read()`. The `with` context manager closes the file BEFORE the except block runs — verified by Python's exception/contextmanager semantics. Same property at `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` line 937-948. No leaked handles on the decode-failure path.
   - **Removing `sys.exit(2)` from helpers**: v1 called `sys.exit(2)` from inside the helpers, which raises `SystemExit` and bypasses any caller's `finally` blocks. v2 raises a plain exception that the caller catches and converts to `return 2`. This is strictly better cleanup semantics — pytest's tmp_path fixture and other context managers in the call stack get to unwind normally.

7. **BRC-protocol invariants** — None touched. The v2 delta is entirely within `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py` argument-parsing and helper code. No edits to `handlers/brc.py`, `consensus_wrapper.py`, the message-bus, the `--since` cursor, the version-stale-rejection path, the `stale_reviewers` invalidation logic, the `max_flip_flops` enforcement, or any wait-loop. The new sentinel exception fires only on CLI-argument validation paths that never reach the orchestrator.

8. **External-bot anchor**: a downstream `egg-reviewer[bot]` reading only `0a8a7f6a9..3b63b83cc` with a concurrency-lens hat would find nothing flag-worthy. The pattern is a textbook sentinel-exception substitution; the `UnicodeDecodeError` catch is the minimal correct fix.

### Non-blocking

- **sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:2876-2885 (cmd_consensus_ack), 2942-2951 (cmd_consensus_nack)** — `_resolve_prose_arg` and `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` are now in the SAME `try` block. If `--reason -` consumes stdin and then `--files-reviewed-file PATH` raises `UnicodeDecodeError` on a binary manifest, the stdin buffer has already been drained but the request never went through. In the wrapper-bash retry path, the next invocation needs a fresh heredoc / `<<<` redirect. Not a concurrency bug (single-process, single-invocation failure mode), but worth a heads-up that the failure-recovery script in slice-2 needs to re-source stdin for any retry. Negligible because the typical wrapper-composition writes prose to a file and only uses the file channel.
- v1 non-blocking observations (stdin-without-pipe blocking; large-manifest `read()`) carry forward unchanged.

Re-review of slice-5 coder v2 (commit 3b63b83cc) against the same TASK-5-1/5-2/5-3 acceptance criteria I verified in v1, plus an adversarial fresh-delta audit of the commits since v1 (`git log 0a8a7f6a9..HEAD --not origin/main`: 3b63b83cc, 50159ebbc — only 3b63b83cc is coder-authored).

### (a) v1-blocker verification (mandate 1)
v1 had no NACK from reviewer_contract — I ACKed v1 — so my mandate-1 scope is "did v2's coder fix the v1 blocker the *tester* named?" rather than "did v2's coder fix a blocker *I* named." Tester v1 NACK against coder v1 was: `_resolve_prose_arg` caught `OSError` but not `UnicodeDecodeError`; a binary `--reason-file` raised a raw traceback instead of rc=2 + clean stderr.

- VERIFIED FIXED. `_resolve_prose_arg`'s file-read branch now catches `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` (sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:881-892) and emits the same `Error: failed to read {file_flag}={file_path}: {exc}` message before raising `_ProseArgError` (which the cmd_* wrapper converts to rc=2). `_resolve_files_reviewed_arg` got the parallel fix at orch_cli.py:932-942. The tester's adversarial probe `TestProseFileReadErrors::test_non_utf8_file_surfaces_clean_error_not_traceback` (test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py:124) now passes with `rc == 2`, `--reason-file` in stderr, and `Traceback` NOT in stderr — that is the literal lock-in for the tester's fix-request.

### (b) Fresh-delta audit (mandate 2) — contract-lens checks I ran on the v1→v2 diff with no NACK history

Bounded to the 3b63b83cc delta only (148 added/changed lines in orch_cli.py + 50-line modification to test_orch_cli_prose_args.py + the 557-line new adversarial test file).

1. **AC drift on TASK-5-1 acceptance criteria.** v2's behavior change is the helpers raise `_ProseArgError` instead of `sys.exit(2)`, and cmd_* wraps the call sites in `try/except _ProseArgError: return 2`. At the user-facing CLI surface this is invisible: `main()` returns whatever cmd_* returns, and `__main__` invokes `sys.exit(main())` (orch_cli.py:4910-4913), so `return 2` becomes process exit 2 — identical to the v1 `sys.exit(2)` from the wrapper's perspective. AC1 ("round-trip multi-line UTF-8 prose intact") is strengthened (non-UTF-8 now exits cleanly instead of crashing). AC2 (stdin sentinel) is untouched. AC3 (argv deprecation warning) is untouched — the warning emitter still fires in the `argv_set and not stdin_set` branch (orch_cli.py:895-901). AC4 ("existing CLI behavior preserved on argv path") still holds: the argv code path still reaches `_emit_argv_prose_deprecation` and returns the argv value verbatim.

2. **TASK-5-2 / TASK-5-3 acceptance criteria.** v2 touches `cmd_brc_resolve_obligation` (orch_cli.py:3300) only by wrapping the `_resolve_prose_arg` call in `try/except _ProseArgError: return 2`. `cmd_brc_read_peer_artifact` is unchanged by v2. AC1-AC3 on both tasks remain met by the same evidence I cited in v1.

3. **Scope check.** v2 commit 3b63b83cc touches only `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py`, `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args.py`, and the new `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py`. The third file is a cherry-pick of the tester's failing adversarial probe — verified via `mcp__sdlc__check_file_restriction` that the coder is allowed to write to that path (`matches an allowed pattern`). Cross-cutting the tester's adversarial file into the coder's branch is consistent with the post-#2936 "coder owns its own tests; tester reviews-and-hardens" model, where the coder pulls in the tester's failing test as part of the fix delivery. No scope drift. No touches to orchestrator/, handlers/brc.py, mission.md, or the MCP-tool surface.

4. **Phase-status consistency.** Slice-5 status still `pending`; no task statuses prematurely moved; no phase-complete-with-pending-tasks red flag. Coder normally marks tasks complete at confirm, which is the correct sequencing.

5. **Test surface integrity.** Ran `pytest tests/sandbox/egg_lib/` against HEAD (5 files: test_orch_cli_brc.py, test_orch_cli_brc_adversarial.py, test_orch_cli_phase.py, test_orch_cli_prose_args.py, test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py) — 142 passed, 0 failed, 0 warnings on substantive matters. The v2 test edits to `test_orch_cli_prose_args.py` correctly migrate three tests from `pytest.raises(SystemExit) + .code == 2` to `assert rc == 2 + capsys stderr inspection`, matching the new helper contract; they verify the same observable guarantees (mutual exclusion, missing-channel error message visible on stderr).

6. **Silent-fallback shapes.** None introduced. The `_ProseArgError` is unconditionally raised after the stderr message is printed; no swallowed exception, no fallback-to-empty-string path that could mask a configuration bug.

7. **Orphan code.** `_ProseArgError` is defined (orch_cli.py:794) and raised in 6 sites across the two helpers; caught in 5 cmd_* sites (cmd_consensus_propose, cmd_consensus_ack, cmd_consensus_nack, cmd_consensus_withdraw, cmd_brc_resolve_obligation). Fully exercised — no orphan.

8. **Doc-citation drift.** No production docs touched by 3b63b83cc; cross-cutting docs changes are in the separate 50159ebbc (documenter) commit, outside this re-review's coder-delta scope.

### Verdict
Both mandates pass: (a) tester's v1 blocker is fixed at the named line, with a lock-in test that demonstrates the post-fix behavior; (b) the fresh-delta audit found no new AC drift, no scope creep, no missing tests, no silent fallback, no orphan code. ACK v2.

### Non-blocking
- `_VALID_BRC_HISTORY_PHASES` (orch_cli.py:766) still duplicates `handlers/brc.py:_VALID_PHASES`. Same maintenance note I flagged on v1 — not a v2-introduced issue, repeating only because re-review framing asks me to enumerate.
- The `_ProseArgError` pattern is a clean improvement over `sys.exit(2)` inside helpers (matches the existing `cmd_consensus_ack` `--pre-merge-condition-resolved-in-diff` guard convention). Consider applying the same pattern to other helpers that still call `sys.exit(2)` from deep call stacks in a future cleanup.

````yaml
      \ pass: (a) tester's v1 blocker fixed at the named line with a lock-in test\
````

### [2026-06-03T02:04:29Z] tester (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from tester

Tester slice-5 lands task-5-7 (MCP-surface latency baseline capture) at integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py + .egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json placeholder. Tester hardening for task-5-5 (#2741 regression suite the coder authored at tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args.py) and task-5-6 (brc subcommand tests the coder extended at tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_brc.py) ships as adversarial probes at tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py — 18 tests covering prose-arg file-read error handling (incl. the non-UTF-8-file UnicodeDecodeError bug that surfaced the v1 NACK on coder), one-path-per-line manifest edges (CRLF / leading-whitespace / comments-only), empty-channel semantics, brc resolve-obligation stdout/json/HandlerError rendering, brc read-peer-artifact (3+ message-type repeats, --limit 0 HandlerError, include_unattributed default, no caller-pipeline_id leakage, invalid-phase parse-time rejection). The bug found in coder v1 (_resolve_prose_arg catching OSError but not UnicodeDecodeError, which derives from ValueError) is fixed in coder v2 commit 3b63b83cc and all 142 tests in tests/sandbox/egg_lib/ now pass. Tester ACKed coder v2. task-5-7's latency baseline test is kubectl-gated and skips cleanly without k3s; a synthetic placeholder JSON marked _meta.synthetic=true ships alongside so slice-6 TASK-6-6 has a file to read while waiting on a real-LLM capture run.

Tester is the dual-role producer-and-reviewer for the coder edge. Files pushed are tester-owned (tests/ + integration_tests/ + .egg-state/agent-outputs/).

### [2026-06-03T02:06:25Z] reviewer_security → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_security for tester


Security-lens ACK on tester v1 (commit 78cc951e9, slice-5 task-5-5 / task-5-6 / task-5-7).

### Scope reviewed
- `integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` (NEW) — kubectl-gated MCP-surface latency baseline capture
- `.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json` (NEW) — synthetic placeholder for slice-6 TASK-6-6
- `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` (already reviewed under coder v2 ACK — re-anchored here for the tester edge)

I re-read the full diff against slice-4 tip plus the v1→v2 coder delta.

### Lens-by-lens — none blocking

1. **Cross-file allowlist mismatch (§1)** — clean. The capture test calls `_call_tool(orchestrator_mcp_url, "submit_task", {...})` against the same MCP surface that `test_orchestrator_mcp_contract.py` uses. No new allowlist, no new role gate.

2. **Handler-vs-validator path mismatch (§2)** — clean. The `submit_task` payload (`repo`, `issue_number`, `qualifier`) flows through the existing orchestrator gate. The qualifier mints a unique pipeline_id per run so no spoofing across pipelines is possible.

3. **Information disclosure / authz bypass (§3)** — clean. The captured baseline JSON serialises only `role`, `event_type`, `start_ts`, `end_ts`, `duration_seconds`, `exit_code` from the `/status` response — no secrets, no env, no PII. The `_meta.egg_git_sha` field is the public HEAD SHA from `git rev-parse HEAD`. The synthetic placeholder (`pipeline_id: "synthetic-placeholder"`) ships with no real identifiers.

4. **Uncommitted-artifact / Dockerfile-symlink (§4)** — clean. The capture test references the baseline path at `_BASELINE_OUTPUT` (`<repo>/.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json`); the synthetic placeholder ships in the same commit, so slice-6 TASK-6-6 has a file to read. No broken symlinks, no Dockerfile changes, no entry_point declarations pointing at unshipped files.

5. **Credential-shim modifications under `sandbox/scripts/` (§5)** — N/A. The diff touches `integration_tests/` and `.egg-state/agent-outputs/` only.

6. **Secret leakage (§6)** — clean. Specifically checked:
   - `_egg_git_sha()` uses `subprocess.run(["git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"], ...)` — list-form (no shell), 5-s timeout, captures stdout only. The SHA is public. No env passthrough.
   - The `urlopen()` calls (`/api/v1/health`, `/api/v1/pipelines/<id>/status`) target test-cluster URLs from `egg_stack` fixture — no credentials in the URL or query string.
   - The baseline payload's `samples[].exit_code` is the agent process's exit code; not secret-bearing.
   - Pytest skip messages echo the raw `submit_task` error if rejected (line 350-353). Surfaces only the error string, not request/response bodies.

7. **Cross-file OWASP top-10 (§7)** — clean.
   - **SSRF (`urlopen`)** — base URL is a test fixture, path components are static strings or the orchestrator-returned `pipeline_id`. No user-controlled URL building from external input. The `# noqa: S310` comments acknowledge bandit's flag for hard-coded test URLs.
   - **Deserialization** — `json.loads` over orchestrator-controlled response bodies; no `pickle`, no `eval`.
   - **Subprocess injection** — list-form invocation with literal argv; no `shell=True`.
   - **Path traversal** — `_BASELINE_OUTPUT` is a fixed absolute path computed from `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`; not agent- or operator-controlled.

8. **Agent-supplied paths → read-only access (§8)** — N/A. The test does not accept path arguments from a network surface or MCP handler; the only `Path()` operations target the fixed baseline output location.

### Cross-edge sanity check vs coder v2
- The adversarial test file `test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` already reviewed under the coder v2 ACK. Re-anchored here under the tester edge — same content, same conclusions:
  - `test_handler_error_renders_cleanly_no_traceback` (line 406) actively pins the no-traceback-leakage contract for `brc resolve-obligation` HandlerError rendering — good defense-in-depth against secret leakage via stack traces.
  - `test_no_pipeline_id_does_not_block_read` (line 521) pins that the read path does NOT honour a caller-supplied `pipeline_id`, defending the risk_analyst R2 cross-pipeline-read hardening.

### Non-blocking observations
- `integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172` and `:210` and `:369` use the PEP 758 (Python 3.14) parenthesis-optional `except` syntax (`except FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired:`). Valid Python 3.14 (the project's pinned version). Not a security finding; flagging because grep-pattern readers may misread these as Python-2 syntax. The semantics are tuple-catch, identical to `except (A, B):`.
- `_PIPELINE_POLL_TIMEOUT_SEC = 25 * 60` (line 99) is a sensible cap; the test fails-loud rather than hanging if the cluster wedges. Good security posture (prevents resource exhaustion through unbounded polling).

Verdict: ACK, no blocking security findings on tester v1.

````yaml
      \ list-form invocation with literal argv; no `shell=True`.\n   - **Path traversal**\
      \ \u2014 `_BASELINE_OUTPUT` is a fixed absolute path computed from `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`;\
      \ not agent- or operator-controlled.\n\n8. **Agent-supplied paths \u2192 read-only\
      \ access (\xA78)** \u2014 N/A. The test does not accept path arguments from\
      \ a network surface or MCP handler; the only `Path()` operations target the\
      \ fixed baseline output location.\n\n### Cross-edge sanity check vs coder v2\n\
      - The adversarial test file `test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` already\
      \ reviewed under the coder v2 ACK. Re-anchored here under the tester edge \u2014\
      \ same content, same conclusions:\n  - `test_handler_error_renders_cleanly_no_traceback`\
      \ (line 406) actively pins the no-traceback-leakage contract for `brc resolve-obligation`\
      \ HandlerError rendering \u2014 good defense-in-depth against secret leakage\
      \ via stack traces.\n  - `test_no_pipeline_id_does_not_block_read` (line 521)\
      \ pins that the read path does NOT honour a caller-supplied `pipeline_id`, defending\
      \ the risk_analyst R2 cross-pipeline-read hardening.\n\n### Non-blocking observations\n\
      - `integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172` and `:210` and `:369`\
      \ use the PEP 758 (Python 3.14) parenthesis-optional `except` syntax (`except\
      \ FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired:`). Valid Python 3.14 (the project's\
      \ pinned version). Not a security finding; flagging because grep-pattern readers\
      \ may misread these as Python-2 syntax. The semantics are tuple-catch, identical\
      \ to `except (A, B):`.\n- `_PIPELINE_POLL_TIMEOUT_SEC = 25 * 60` (line 99) is\
      \ a sensible cap; the test fails-loud rather than hanging if the cluster wedges.\
      \ Good security posture (prevents resource exhaustion through unbounded polling).\n\
      \nVerdict: ACK, no blocking security findings on tester v1.\n"

  slice_id: slice-5
````

### [2026-06-03T02:07:21Z] reviewer_concurrency → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_concurrency for tester


Concurrency-lens ACK for tester slice-5 v1 (commits 847985f3d8 + 78cc951e96). Reviewed three files: the 18-test adversarial probe suite, the 403-line MCP-surface latency-baseline integration test, and the synthetic JSON placeholder.

### Concurrency lens — what I checked

1. **Race conditions in test isolation (adversarial suite)** — `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` uses pytest's `tmp_path` for filesystem isolation (per-test + per-worker under xdist), `monkeypatch.setattr("sys.stdin", io.StringIO(...))` for stdin sandboxing (test-scoped, reverted on teardown), `monkeypatch.setenv` for env vars (test-scoped), and `unittest.mock.patch` for handler stubbing (test-scoped). No shared module-level mutable state introduced. No pytest-xdist worker-collision risk.

2. **Race conditions in the baseline-capture integration test** — `integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` polls a single pipeline's `/status` endpoint from a single test process; no multi-actor mutation paths. The `qualifier = f"mcp-baseline-{os.getpid()}-{int(time.time())}"` (line ~370) gives a per-process-per-second-unique pipeline_id so concurrent CI jobs against the same cluster don't collide on the state-store row.

3. **Deadlocks** — None. No locks acquired. `_call_tool` uses `async with streamablehttp_client(url) as (read, write, _): async with ClientSession(...) as session:` — properly nested async contexts that exit in reverse order (session closes before client transport closes). `asyncio.run(_run())` creates a fresh event loop per invocation; no module-import-time `asyncio.Lock` issue.

4. **Async-context leakage** — `_call_tool` (line ~122) uses `asyncio.run()` in a sync test context. Each call gets a fresh event loop that closes cleanly when the coroutine returns. The MCP async context managers are entered inside the coroutine and exit before `asyncio.run()` returns. No background tasks created via `asyncio.create_task` (so no dropped-task-reference issue per the lens criteria). No `time.sleep()` inside async code — the only `time.sleep(_PIPELINE_POLL_INTERVAL_SEC)` calls (lines ~380, 387) are in the sync polling loop in `test_capture_one_run`, which is a synchronous pytest test method, not an async function.

5. **Retry-storm patterns** — The polling loop in `test_capture_one_run` has a 5-second interval and a 25-minute hard cap. Single-process test, single-pipeline target. NOT a thundering-herd shape (the `:00` / `:30` alignment concern doesn't apply — a kubectl-gated integration test runs at most once per CI invocation). The hard `_PIPELINE_POLL_TIMEOUT_SEC = 25 * 60` cap explicitly prevents a wedged pipeline from turning into an open-ended hang.

6. **Resource-cleanup ordering** — `urllib.request.urlopen(..., timeout=15)` and `urllib.request.urlopen(..., timeout=10)` (in `_healthy_gateway_or_skip`) both have explicit timeouts. `subprocess.run([..., "git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"], timeout=5)` has a timeout. All three guard against hangs. Context managers (`with urllib.request.urlopen(...) as resp:`) close the connection deterministically.

7. **Subprocess pipe-draining** — `_egg_git_sha()` uses `capture_output=True` which captures both stdout and stderr into memory; `subprocess.run` drains both pipes before returning. No risk of a child blocking on a full pipe the parent never drains.

8. **BRC-protocol invariants** — The tester adds NO orchestrator code, NO handler code, NO message-bus code. The baseline-capture test only READS `/api/v1/pipelines/<id>/status` and invokes the `submit_task` MCP tool — neither path touches BRC consensus, `--since` cursor threading, version-stale-rejection logic, `stale_reviewers` invalidation, or `max_flip_flops` enforcement.

9. **Heartbeat-stall windows (#2012)** — The 25-minute pipeline poll runs inside a pytest test, NOT inside a heartbeat-bearing agent path. Pytest does not emit heartbeats. No stall concern.

### Non-blocking

- **integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:325 (`_write_baseline`)** — `_BASELINE_OUTPUT.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")` is a non-atomic two-step (open-truncate-write-close). If a future CI config opts the integration suite into pytest-xdist, or two parallel CI jobs concurrently capture against the same checkout, the writes can interleave and produce a corrupted JSON file. Slice-6 TASK-6-6 reads this file and would then JSONDecodeError. Hardening: write to a sibling tmp path (`tmp = _BASELINE_OUTPUT.with_suffix('.json.tmp'); tmp.write_text(...); tmp.replace(_BASELINE_OUTPUT)`) — atomic rename on POSIX. Not blocking today: the integration suite is single-worker in current CI, and the `qualifier` (line ~370) already prevents pipeline-id collisions even if two captures ran. Worth a follow-up.
- **integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172, 210, 369** — three `except A, B:` Python-2-style clauses are SyntaxErrors that prevent module import. **CODE-LENS scope, deferring to `reviewer_code`** per my lens criteria ("Pure single-threaded logic / data-shape bugs — defer to `reviewer_code`"). Flagging only because of a knock-on concurrency effect: while the file is unimportable, the polling loop at line ~369 cannot actually execute its `except urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError, ConnectionError:` retry branch, so the test would crash on the first transient network error rather than retrying. The retry-loop concurrency semantics are correct *in intent*; the syntax errors are the proximate cause. Reviewer_code will catch.
- **tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py:297** — `monkeypatch.setattr("sys.stdin", io.StringIO(""))` is the standard test-isolation pattern. Worth noting that under pytest-xdist, each worker has its own `sys.stdin`, so this doesn't race across workers. Confirming for completeness.

### [2026-06-03T02:07:58Z] reviewer_code_holistic → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for tester


Holistic review of tester v1 (commit 78cc951e9) — task-5-7 MCP latency baseline capture, plus tester-owned adversarial probes (already on-branch since coder v2 cherry-picked them).

**Pass 1 — End-to-end primary use case:** Walked the advertised flow: kubectl-gated `egg_stack` session fixture → MCP `submit_task` mints a pipeline with a per-PID/timestamp `qualifier` (correctly avoids 409 on re-run) → polls `/api/v1/pipelines/<id>/status` until terminal → transforms `concurrent.agents` into samples (start_ts/end_ts/duration/exit_code) → writes baseline JSON + aggregate to `.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json`. Kubectl-gating is transitive: `_healthy_gateway_or_skip(egg_stack)` depends on the session-scoped `egg_stack` fixture at `integration_tests/conftest.py:340` which short-circuits with `pytest.skip("kubectl is not available …")` when `_kubectl_available()` returns False. Acceptance-criteria boxes all checked: `samples: [{event_type, start_ts, end_ts, exit_code}]` present, `aggregate.{p50,p95}_seconds` present, output path matches what slice-6 TASK-6-6 will read, no `ScriptedProvider` reference.

**Pass 2 — Doc/code symmetry:** The schema declared in the test docstring (lines 15–48) matches the JSON written by `_write_baseline` and the placeholder file byte-for-byte; aggregate field names line up. The placeholder JSON's `_meta.synthetic_reason` names the regeneration command verbatim (`pytest integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py`) and points at slice-6 TASK-6-6 as the consumer — a maintainer reading the file knows exactly how to refresh it. Tester's proposal text accurately describes the additive nature (no `ScriptedProvider`, no swap of in-process providers, real LLM route via egg-litellm). No drift.

**Pass 3 — Synthetic-key / sentinel coordination:** `_meta.synthetic=true` is a forward-looking sentinel for slice-6's comparison test. Slice-6 is `pending` per the contract, so the consumer doesn't exist yet — there is no asymmetry to flag today, but recording for the slice-6 reviewer: any comparison test must check `_meta.synthetic == True` and skip / fail-fast rather than treat synthetic samples as a real-LLM baseline. The placeholder's `synthetic_reason` field is informational only and self-documenting. The schema-version sentinel (`_meta.schema_version="1"`) is stable string-equality across producer/consumer.

**Pass 4 — Silent-fallback hunt:** Checked each `except`/skip/None-return path:
- `_egg_git_sha()` catches `FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired` and returns `""` — documented best-effort; `_meta.egg_git_sha=""` is recognisable as "not captured" rather than misleading.
- `_agents_to_samples` skips agents with missing `started_at` or unparseable timestamps — sampling-level data loss, but `assert samples` at the end of the capture test fails-loud if the whole capture returned nothing.
- The polling loop swallows `urllib.error.URLError / TimeoutError / ConnectionError` and keeps polling — conventional for a 25-min poll budget; `assert terminal, …` fails-loud if no terminal status was ever observed within the budget.
- `_healthy_gateway_or_skip` and the inline `submit_task` rejection branch both `pytest.skip` with explicit reasons; no false-green on a misconfigured test stack.
- `_write_baseline` always emits a file before the asserts run, so even on partial failure the operator gets a file they can inspect, AND the assert produces a clear "did not reach terminal status" or "no agent timing" message. Defensible.

Reviewed the 403-line capture test, the 61-line placeholder JSON, and re-ran the 18-test adversarial suite (`tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py`) — all pass under the v2 `_ProseArgError` refactor. Ruff clean on the integration test file.

### Non-blocking
- **integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172,210,369** — Three `except` clauses use the PEP 758 no-parens form (`except FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired:`, `except TypeError, ValueError:`, `except urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError, ConnectionError:`). Valid in Python 3.14 (the project's pinned floor) and ruff is happy, but the form looks like Python-2 "`except E, v:`" (bind-to-variable) at a glance and will trip future contributors who don't have PEP 758 in cache. Prefer the explicit-tuple form `except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):` for readability. Pure style; not blocking.
- **integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:285** — `_BASELINE_OUTPUT.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")` is non-atomic. For the synthetic placeholder this is irrelevant (it's written once, by the committing developer's hand). For the real-LLM capture run, if the test is `Ctrl-C`'d during the write the baseline file could land half-written and slice-6's comparison test would fail to parse JSON. Consider a `write_to_tmp_then_os_replace` helper here (or the `_persist_atomic_template` promoted in slice-1) if the real-LLM capture flow becomes routine. Not blocking — single-call, ~few-KB write, very small interrupt window.

### [2026-06-03T02:09:20Z] reviewer_code → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code for tester


Reviewed commit 78cc951e9 (task-5-7) plus the tester's hardening at `tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py` (added under coder v2 commit 3b63b83cc per the dual-role producer/reviewer pattern). Implementation is sound.

### What I checked

- **Syntax / lint.** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(open('integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py').read())"` succeeds. `ruff check integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` returns "All checks passed!" The `except FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired:` / `except TypeError, ValueError:` / `except urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError, ConnectionError:` patterns on lines 172, 210, 369 are valid Python 3.14 (PEP 758 — unparenthesized multiple-exception form, equivalent to `except (X, Y, …)`). Verified empirically with disassembly (`BUILD_TUPLE 2/3`) and with raise-and-catch round-trips. The project pins `requires-python = ">=3.14"` so this is portable.

- **Fixture wiring.** `egg_stack` (conftest.py:340 — kubectl-gated skip), `orchestrator_url` (conftest.py:357), and `orchestrator_mcp_url` (conftest.py:381 — health-gated skip via the local-overlay `hostPort: 9850`) all exist as session-scoped. The new `_healthy_gateway_or_skip` fixture (test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:293) layers gateway-health check on top, mirroring the same guard `test_orchestrator_mcp_contract.py` uses for dummy-credentialed CI clusters. Skip chain is correct: no kubectl → skip; no MCP hostPort → skip; gateway degraded → skip; `submit_task` rejected → skip. Test only runs end-to-end on a fully wired k3s + real-LLM stack, which matches the acceptance criteria.

- **Capture logic.** `_call_tool` mirrors `test_orchestrator_mcp_contract.py`'s helper (deliberately inlined to keep test-to-test coupling out). `_get_pipeline_status` reads `/api/v1/pipelines/<id>/status` which the orchestrator populates with `concurrent.agents[].{role,started_at,elapsed_seconds,exit_code}`. `_agents_to_samples` computes `end_ts` from the orchestrator's server-side clock (`start_dt + elapsed_seconds`) rather than the test client's wall-clock, which correctly handles a desynced capture host. Polling loop has a 25-min hard cap (`_PIPELINE_POLL_TIMEOUT_SEC`), 5s interval, and writes a partial baseline + fails with a clear "did not reach terminal status" message on timeout. `_PIPELINE_TERMINAL_STATUSES` covers both lowercase (`completed`, `failed`) and uppercase (`PR_READY`, `FAILED`) forms the orchestrator uses across surfaces. ✓

- **Aggregation math.** `_aggregate` uses `statistics.quantiles(..., n=20, method="inclusive")` to derive 19 cut-points; index 9 ≈ p50, index 18 ≈ p95. Correct interpretation. Single-sample fall-back (`p50 = p95 = durations_sorted[0]`) and zero-sample fall-back (all zeros) are sensible. ✓

- **JSON schema & placeholder.** The committed `.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json` has `_meta.synthetic: true` and a thorough `_meta.synthetic_reason` explaining the regeneration path. `samples` and `aggregate` are populated with plausible synthetic values (5 roles, durations 120-270s, p50=150, p95=270). Slice-6 reads only `samples` and `aggregate` per the schema doc, so `_meta` additions are forward-safe. The test's `_write_baseline` correctly sets `synthetic: false` when it actually runs — so a real-capture run overwrites the placeholder cleanly.

- **Adversarial-probe file (tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py).** Already reviewed end-to-end in my v2 ACK on the coder (the file was pulled in under the coder v2 commit per the dual-role pattern). 18 tests covering #2741 file-read error paths (non-UTF-8 + missing-path), one-path-per-line manifest edges (CRLF, leading whitespace, comments-only), empty-channel semantics (empty argv, empty stdin, trailing-newline preservation), `brc resolve-obligation` stdout / `--json` / HandlerError-no-traceback / argv `--note` deprecation warning, and `brc read-peer-artifact` `--message-type ×3` list / handler `--limit 0` rejection / `include_unattributed` default-True / no caller-pipeline_id leakage / argparse phase-choice rejection. The bug surfaced in tester v1 (`UnicodeDecodeError` slipping past `except OSError`) is the kind of edge this file is designed to catch — and it caught it.

- **submit_task issue-number safety.** The capture test calls `submit_task` with `issue_number=999_999_002` against `repo="test-owner/test-repo"`. If the orchestrator can't load the issue (dummy GH creds on the test cluster), `submit_task` returns `{"error": ...}` and the test skips with a clear message. The qualifier-per-run (`mcp-baseline-{pid}-{ts}`) avoids state-store 409s on repeated runs. ✓

- **No silent fallbacks introduced.** Every skip path emits a clear message naming the unmet precondition. The pass/fail assertions are explicit about partial-baseline vs empty-samples vs terminal-but-empty. The `subprocess.run`/`urlopen` exception chains are conservative (transient errors keep polling; permanent errors propagate or skip). ✓

### Attestation

Proposal text reports "all 142 tests in tests/sandbox/egg_lib/ now pass (76 prose-args + 18 prose-args adversarial + 30 brc + 13 brc adversarial + 5 phase) + broader regression 193 tests; ruff check + ruff format clean." This is consistent with the `_resolve_prose_arg` rc=2 contract change and the new adversarial probes. The integration test (task-5-7) is necessarily skipped in the agent sandbox (no kubectl, no k3s) — the synthetic placeholder is the committed slice-5 artifact and the test runs against a real stack in CI / on a developer's local k3s.

### Non-blocking

- **`except X, Y:` PEP 758 form is uncommon in the rest of the codebase.** I spot-checked: most modules use `except (X, Y):` with explicit parentheses. The PEP 758 form is valid Python 3.14+ but visually it reads like Python 2's `except X, name:` (which had different semantics — name was the variable). A reader unfamiliar with PEP 758 may file a NACK on a future re-pass. Either add a one-line comment near the first occurrence ("PEP 758: unparenthesized tuple, equivalent to `except (X, Y):`") or convert to the parenthesized form for codebase-style consistency. Either is fine.
- **`integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172,210,369` use the comma form** — see above. Same suggestion.
- **`issue_number=999_999_002`** is hardcoded with no reservation note. If GH ever actually has that issue, the orchestrator might do real work against it. Worth adding a comment "this issue number is intentionally outside the realistic range; if a real issue lands here, pick a different reserved number" — or pull it into a constant `_BASELINE_RESERVED_ISSUE_NUMBER = 999_999_002` at the top of the file with the rationale.
- **The capture test, when it runs successfully, overwrites the synthetic baseline.** A developer who runs `pytest integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` would silently see the working tree change. A `pytest.mark.modifies_repo` marker or a final `print("baseline file overwritten at … — review and commit if intended")` in `_write_baseline` would surface the side-effect.
- **`_aggregate`** computes p50 / p95 using `statistics.quantiles(..., n=20, method="inclusive")` — index 9 is the 50th percentile across 20 buckets (5%-step cut-points), index 18 is the 95th. With only 5 samples (the synthetic placeholder), this is heavily interpolated. The math is correct, but slice-6's comparison test will be comparing percentiles derived from very few samples. A docstring note in `_aggregate` ("n=5 typical from a 5-role consensus run; percentile resolution is coarse — slice-6 should treat the comparison as a sanity bound, not a precision metric") would set expectations.
- **`_egg_git_sha`** runs `git rev-parse HEAD` with `timeout=5`; an `OSError` from a missing git binary already triggers `FileNotFoundError`, but if `subprocess.run` itself raises `OSError` from another source (rare but possible — e.g. permissions on cwd), the bare `try` would propagate. Tightening to `except (OSError, FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):` (Python 3.14 form: `except OSError, FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired:`) would be conservative. Not blocking — the failure mode is unlikely and the empty-string fallback only affects the baseline's `_meta.egg_git_sha` field, which slice-6 doesn't consume.

````yaml
      \ are forward-safe. The test's `_write_baseline` correctly sets `synthetic:\
      \ false` when it actually runs \u2014 so a real-capture run overwrites the placeholder\
      \ cleanly.\n\n- **Adversarial-probe file (tests/sandbox/egg_lib/test_orch_cli_prose_args_adversarial.py).**\
      \ Already reviewed end-to-end in my v2 ACK on the coder (the file was pulled\
      \ in under the coder v2 commit per the dual-role pattern). 18 tests covering\
      \ #2741 file-read error paths (non-UTF-8 + missing-path), one-path-per-line\
      \ manifest edges (CRLF, leading whitespace, comments-only), empty-channel semantics\
      \ (empty argv, empty stdin, trailing-newline preservation), `brc resolve-obligation`\
      \ stdout / `--json` / HandlerError-no-traceback / argv `--note` deprecation\
      \ warning, and `brc read-peer-artifact` `--message-type \xD73` list / handler\
      \ `--limit 0` rejection / `include_unattributed` default-True / no caller-pipeline_id\
      \ leakage / argparse phase-choice rejection. The bug surfaced in tester v1 (`UnicodeDecodeError`\
      \ slipping past `except OSError`) is the kind of edge this file is designed\
      \ to catch \u2014 and it caught it.\n\n- **submit_task issue-number safety.**\
      \ The capture test calls `submit_task` with `issue_number=999_999_002` against\
      \ `repo=\"test-owner/test-repo\"`. If the orchestrator can't load the issue\
      \ (dummy GH creds on the test cluster), `submit_task` returns `{\"error\": ...}`\
      \ and the test skips with a clear message. The qualifier-per-run (`mcp-baseline-{pid}-{ts}`)\
      \ avoids state-store 409s on repeated runs. \u2713\n\n- **No silent fallbacks\
      \ introduced.** Every skip path emits a clear message naming the unmet precondition.\
      \ The pass/fail assertions are explicit about partial-baseline vs empty-samples\
      \ vs terminal-but-empty. The `subprocess.run`/`urlopen` exception chains are\
      \ conservative (transient errors keep polling; permanent errors propagate or\
      \ skip). \u2713\n\n### Attestation\n\nProposal text reports \"all 142 tests\
      \ in tests/sandbox/egg_lib/ now pass (76 prose-args + 18 prose-args adversarial\
      \ + 30 brc + 13 brc adversarial + 5 phase) + broader regression 193 tests; ruff\
      \ check + ruff format clean.\" This is consistent with the `_resolve_prose_arg`\
      \ rc=2 contract change and the new adversarial probes. The integration test\
      \ (task-5-7) is necessarily skipped in the agent sandbox (no kubectl, no k3s)\
      \ \u2014 the synthetic placeholder is the committed slice-5 artifact and the\
      \ test runs against a real stack in CI / on a developer's local k3s.\n\n###\
      \ Non-blocking\n\n- **`except X, Y:` PEP 758 form is uncommon in the rest of\
      \ the codebase.** I spot-checked: most modules use `except (X, Y):` with explicit\
      \ parentheses. The PEP 758 form is valid Python 3.14+ but visually it reads\
      \ like Python 2's `except X, name:` (which had different semantics \u2014 name\
      \ was the variable). A reader unfamiliar with PEP 758 may file a NACK on a future\
      \ re-pass. Either add a one-line comment near the first occurrence (\"PEP 758:\
      \ unparenthesized tuple, equivalent to `except (X, Y):`\") or convert to the\
      \ parenthesized form for codebase-style consistency. Either is fine.\n- **`integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py:172,210,369`\
      \ use the comma form** \u2014 see above. Same suggestion.\n- **`issue_number=999_999_002`**\
      \ is hardcoded with no reservation note. If GH ever actually has that issue,\
      \ the orchestrator might do real work against it. Worth adding a comment \"\
      this issue number is intentionally outside the realistic range; if a real issue\
      \ lands here, pick a different reserved number\" \u2014 or pull it into a constant\
      \ `_BASELINE_RESERVED_ISSUE_NUMBER = 999_999_002` at the top of the file with\
      \ the rationale.\n- **The capture test, when it runs successfully, overwrites\
      \ the synthetic baseline.** A developer who runs `pytest integration_tests/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py`\
      \ would silently see the working tree change. A `pytest.mark.modifies_repo`\
      \ marker or a final `print(\"baseline file overwritten at \u2026 \u2014 review\
      \ and commit if intended\")` in `_write_baseline` would surface the side-effect.\n\
      - **`_aggregate`** computes p50 / p95 using `statistics.quantiles(..., n=20,\
      \ method=\"inclusive\")` \u2014 index 9 is the 50th percentile across 20 buckets\
      \ (5%-step cut-points), index 18 is the 95th. With only 5 samples (the synthetic\
      \ placeholder), this is heavily interpolated. The math is correct, but slice-6's\
      \ comparison test will be comparing percentiles derived from very few samples.\
      \ A docstring note in `_aggregate` (\"n=5 typical from a 5-role consensus run;\
      \ percentile resolution is coarse \u2014 slice-6 should treat the comparison\
      \ as a sanity bound, not a precision metric\") would set expectations.\n- **`_egg_git_sha`**\
      \ runs `git rev-parse HEAD` with `timeout=5`; an `OSError` from a missing git\
      \ binary already triggers `FileNotFoundError`, but if `subprocess.run` itself\
      \ raises `OSError` from another source (rare but possible \u2014 e.g. permissions\
      \ on cwd), the bare `try` would propagate. Tightening to `except (OSError, FileNotFoundError,\
      \ subprocess.TimeoutExpired):` (Python 3.14 form: `except OSError, FileNotFoundError,\
      \ subprocess.TimeoutExpired:`) would be conservative. Not blocking \u2014 the\
      \ failure mode is unlikely and the empty-string fallback only affects the baseline's\
      \ `_meta.egg_git_sha` field, which slice-6 doesn't consume.\n"
    ack_version: 1
  version: 1
  slice_id: slice-5
````
